# Synmergia - Developer Notes for AI Agents

> **Purpose:** This file is a **stable reference** for AI agents
> working on Synmergia. It documents code conventions, architecture,
> and patterns that don't change frequently.
>
> **What this file is NOT:** An operational log, debugging status,
> or session history. (Session notes go in `labs/TASKS.md`.)

> **Root docs:** [README.md](./README.md) (product + architecture) ·

> [DEPLOY-GPU.md](./DEPLOY-GPU.md) (3D pipeline) · [SECURITY.md](./SECURITY.md) ·

> **Last updated 2026-11-04 (invisible-wall fix - the terrain-conforming surface pad):** THE "INVISIBLE WALL" / "WALK SURFACE != TERRAIN" REPORT IS CLOSED. THE CAUSE: at planet scale the ground collider was a FLAT 64 m disc (`globe_world.SurfacePad`) anchored at the pad origin's lift (`planet_radius + TILE_LIFT + terrain_h(player_dir)`), while the walk surface follows the heightfield -- so wherever the ground rose faster than the flat disc the pad stood PROUD of the next rise and stopped the body dead (measured hard stop at arc ~507 m; the walk metric showed the body rising ~0.238 m then `moved=0.000`). THE FIX (`globe_world._update_surface_pad` + new `_terrain_pad_faces`): rebuild the pad's `ConcavePolygonShape3D` from the WALK-SURFACE LAW under the player -- a centre vertex + `PAD_RINGS` rings out to `PAD_RADIUS`, each vertex's local +Y = `walk_surface_h(its own radial dir)` minus the origin terrain (the origin already rides `_surface_lift`), with the per-ring sphere sagitta subtracted so the disc hugs curvature. Local coords stay <= PAD_RADIUS (physics needs small shape coords). Rebuild is throttled by `_pad_built_dir` (only when the player moves > 3 m). TWO REAL BUGS CAUGHT IN THE FIRST CUT: (1) the innermost ring was radius 0 (a degenerate ring left a HOLE at the pad centre the body fell through -- replaced with a centre vertex + fan); (2) `_terrain_pad_faces` built the sample direction as `center + basis*local` where `center` was the UNIT direction -- so `center*planet_radius` was missing and every sample read the law ~90 deg away (the pad sampled a completely different part of the world; this was ALSO the root cause of the long-standing `probe_planet_walk_20km` snap-OFF stage regression: with the bug the snap-OFF walk stalled at ~7-8 m, with the scale fix it walks 20.1 m). RECEIPT `tests/probe_long_walk.gd` (new, pass/fail): walks a great circle in 8 m steps and raycasts the pad against the DRAWN ground (`walk_surface_h + TILE_LIFT`) under the body and 20 m ahead -- PASS err centre 0.05 m / err ahead ~1.2 m over 1200 m (rocks/props on the same layer are filtered by `collider != pad`). The real auto-walk reaches 2000 m clean (was dead-stopped ~507 m). Snap-ON and snap-OFF 20 km stages both pass. Gates: probe_long_walk PASS, probe_planet_walk_20km 15/0, probe_walk_mesh_gap (pad tracks drawn ground), probe_prop_density green.



> **Last updated 2026-11-11 (round-7 harvest + parent fixes):** THE CONTENT-REF GUARD IS BORN. `tests/test_content_ref_integrity.gd` (7 legs, runner DEFAULT_SET) refuses the silent-break class no receipt covered: every loot-table `entries[].item_id` resolves through DataService (with `book_<skill>_<tier>` validated as skill+tier, the GroundLootService runtime expansion), every loot `currency_id` resolves, every vendor offering `catalog_items[].item_id`/`currency_id` resolves, and every enemy `loot_table_id`/`secondary_loot_table_id` names a real table. TWO SILENT BUGS it caught: `loot_bandit.tres` declared its gold drop INSIDE `entries` (a currency row in the item list -> an empty item_id at runtime, so the bandit's 5-20 gold NEVER dropped; moved to `currency_drops`), and `LootTable.roll_drop` keyed gold by whatever the data spelled -- `"gold"` x5 but `"currency_gold"` x1 -- while the consumers (`enemy_data.roll_loot`, `GroundLootService`) read gold as `"gold"`, so a `currency_gold` table's coins vanished; normalized at the source. MINIMAP VENDOR MARKER: the MARKER_VENDOR branch read `npc_data.get("services")` -- a phantom NPCData never declared, so the read always returned `[]` and the vendor branch was UNREACHABLE; now `NPCEntity.is_vendor()` (resolved from the real `includes[]` offering model, ref-based or inline) + a `_npc_marker_type()` classifier (receipt `test_minimap_vendor_marker.gd`). LANE ROUND 7 (K3 data phantom-close + H3 terrain + L gear shelves) HARVESTED: **H3 closed OPEN DEFECT (b)** -- the face-16 "vertex-mesh corruption" was a PROBE READER artifact (the spawn direction is the midpoint of the icosa EDGE between vertices 8 and 10, a vertex of faces 16 and 18 only, NOT a 5-face vertex; the old crumple rows came from the reader sampling an out-of-tree patch with identity XF). A new planet-wide all-face mesh-law sweep reads worst 0.0068 m, 0 bad (face 16 = 0.003 m); NO production mesh code changed. **L filled the gear shelves**: class signature gear (one weapon + one armor per playable class, gm_* excluded), the missing enum types (crossbow/wand/bracelet), a high-tier level ladder, two high-tier loot tables wired to the elite + boss that had NONE, a shop-grid GROW (`ShopUI._ensure_slot_count` -- a by-category shelf past 16 rows was silently truncated so most armory/general-store stock was unseeable), and the out-of-range shield `weapon_type` fixed (11 -> 10). LANE LORE: an infra 503 (OpenAIError: Service Temporarily Unavailable) killed BOTH round-7 lanes mid-run with nothing committed; resume via `labs/lane_spawn.sh send <conv> <resume-brief.md>` (a resumed lane keeps its uncommitted worktree edits). Gates on the final HEAD: content ref 7/0, naming lint 30/0, item catalog 10/0, item use 36/0, minimap 7/0, probe_ground_law_audit ALL PASS, probe_terrain_planet PASS, terrain determinism byte-stable.
> **Last updated 2026-11-04 (agent-play - THE AGENT BODY, the keystone):** THE KEYSTONE GAP IS CLOSED -- an agent is a REAL PLAYER now, with its own body. THE GAP: "an agent is a player" held in every layer EXCEPT the body -- `globe_world.gd::_build_player()` built exactly ONE player body, `agent_driver.gd` acted on `_local_player()` -> `PlayerManager.local_player`, and an agent's character id resolved to NO live body, so an agent could only ever be a test double. THE SPINE (shared, single-writer): (1) `entities/globe_player.gd` gained PER-BODY VIRTUAL INPUT -- `set_virtual_input(move: Vector2, sprint: bool)` / `clear_virtual_input()` / `has_virtual_input()`; movement + sprint read the virtual stick INSTEAD of the global `Input` singleton when live, so two bodies share one process without steering each other (convention: x = strafe right, y = forward; a zero vector still counts as virtual-ON so an agent never falls back to the human's keys). DRIVE-BY: only a LOCAL body claims the `PlayerManager` seat -- `_ready` gates on `is_local_player` and `_adopt_local_seat.call_deferred()` re-claims it a frame later, so an agent body added AFTER the human can never steal the seat (the agent body sets `is_local_player = false` BEFORE `add_child`, so `_ready` is already correct). (2) `core/services/agent_driver.gd` is PRINCIPAL-aware: `act(id, {principal})` routes verbs to the body owned by that handle; `observe(principal)` reports THAT body (and echoes `principal`); `move`/`stop` set the body's virtual stick (the human's body keeps riding the real action map); a principal-less act still drives the human's seat. An explicit principal that does NOT resolve is REFUSED BY NAME (`unknown principal '<h>' (no live agent body)`) -- NEVER a silent fall-back onto the human (the cross-body drive Lane I's door receipt caught: a ghost principal used to return ok:true AND drive the human). `""` / `local` / `human` resolve to the human by design. (3) `core/services/agent_body_service.gd` (NEW autoload `AgentBodyService`, after `AgentRoster`): owns the BODY. `spawn(account_id, handle, opts)` builds a real `GlobeWorldPlayer` in the human's world (inheriting the walk law: same planet_center/radius/ground_offset/snap provider), adds it to the tree FIRST (register reads global_position), registers with `EntityService`, and links it into `AgentCoordinator.link_entity(handle, entity_id)`; `body_for(handle)` / `board()` / `despawn(handle)`. `_world()` requires a REAL globe (`terrain_h_at` seam) -- a unit probe parenting a fabricated body to the tree root does not read as a world. `despawn` keeps the coordinator's `entity_id` (the agent's persistent CHARACTER, not the transient body). (4) `core/services/agent_roster.gd`: `seat_agent` spawns a body when none (falling back to id-only seating when no body service is mounted -- the legacy unit path); `despawn_agent` routes through the body service ONLY when it owns the body, else removes directly; NEW `re_enable` / `re_enable_all` (the sec 17.2 manual re-enable UX -- agents do NOT auto-respawn; a stale party seat is unseated first). (5) `core/services/agent_door.gd` passes `principal` through `/act` and `/observe`. RECEIPTS: `tests/test_agent_body_lifecycle.gd` 30/0 (in-process: a real body in the same world, the two-input law, the agent WALKS 3.16 m under its own input, the human untouched, despawn banks+removes, re-enable respawns + refuses by name while AFK, observe(principal) reports the agent's own body); `tests/test_agent_body_door_spec.gd` 31/0/0 gaps (Lane I: the SAME proof over the real gamestruct HTTP wire, incl. the unknown-principal refusal). LANE HARVEST (2 subagent lanes, briefs `labs/lanes/lane-{I,J}.md`, cherry-picked): Lane I found the unknown-principal fall-back (fixed in the parent); Lane J shipped the AGENT ROSTER PANEL (`ui/components/agent_roster_ui.gd` on the MenuManager "agents" row -- rows read `AgentRoster.board`, a Re-enable button rides `re_enable`, refresh rides the roster's own signals) AND found a SHARED shell bug: `MenuManager.open_menu()` flipped `visible` but never called `open()`, so any LAZILY-built panel opened through the static facade (ignore/channels/premium) rendered an EMPTY SHELL -- fixed in the parent (`_open_menu_internal` now routes through the panel's `open()`, mirroring `_open_panel`). DRIVE-BY: `MenuManager` panels are built in `_initialize_panels` (not `_setup`). Gates: lint 37/0, naming 27/0, agent_roster 38/0, party_bridge 32/0, coordinator 30/0, activity 14/0, login 30/0, ignore_book ok, premium ok, channel_mute ok. NOTE: `test_agent_driver.gd` shows 29/0 with 2 display-only `see()` legs failing headless (the test documents `xvfb-run`; no framebuffer).
> **Last updated 2026-11-04 (lane round 4 - the live-spawn placeholder bodies):** THE ENEMY MODELS THAT MATTER. THE FINDING: the "23 enemies with empty `model_path`" headline OVERSTATES the live impact -- only FOUR enemies actually spawn in the live globe (`GLOBE_ENEMY_BATCH` in `scenes/worlds/globe_world.gd`: `wolf` x2, `thistle_boar`, `training_dummy`), and an empty `model_path` means a PLACEHOLDER BOX body (`entities/enemy.gd` mounts the .glb only when `model_path` is set; the phantom-path lint keeps it empty until the file exists). TWO of those four live spawns were placeholder boxes; the other 21 are authored-but-unspawned (a later placement question, out of scope). THREE LANES AUTHORED THE MISSING BODIES end-to-end through the Blender generator pipeline (each: `scripts/gen_<id>_model.py` deterministic -> `game/models/enemies/<id>.glb` -> one `model_path` line -> a `probe_<id>_model.gd` receipt): **Lane E** `enemy_bandit` (humanoid, reuses the blacksmith biped rig, idle+walk, 292 KB), **Lane F** `enemy_thistle_boar` (quadruped off the wolf rig, thistle-matting ridge, idle+walk, 287 KB), **Lane G** `enemy_training_dummy` (static straw target, NO rig, 37 KB). All three HARVESTED into `staging` by cherry-pick (each lane = one commit on disjoint files, no conflicts); their probes join the runner DEFAULT_SET (`probe_bandit_model`, `probe_training_dummy_model`, `probe_thistle_boar_model`). THE LANE-ACCESS ANSWER: a lane worktree is a synmergia checkout with NO `node_modules/` (gitignored), so `import 'gamestruct'` FAILS from the repo root -- but the lane CAN read the sibling SOURCE repos directly (`/workspace/gamestruct` v1.1.0 incl. `lib/adapters/synmergia.mjs`, and `/workspace/vant`). `labs/lane_setup.sh` (run FIRST in a lane) closes both fresh-worktree gaps: symlinks `node_modules/gamestruct` -> `/workspace/gamestruct` and warms the Godot import cache. LANE LORE: UV-sphere mesh exports are NONDETERMINISTIC in this Blender 4.3.2 build (index-buffer order; the pre-existing wolf/slime carry it) -- a byte-deterministic generator uses box/cone/cylinder only (the boar did); and a mesh part named the same as a bone COLLIDES (the importer renames the bone `neck` -> `neck_2`) -- rename the part (`neck_mass`). Verification is structural (import + probe: real mesh/material/rig/clips), never a render. Gates: `test_data_naming_lint` 27/0 + the three new model probes all green through the runner (4 named tests: 4 passed/0, 6s at 4 jobs).

> **Last updated 2026-11-04 (lane round 3 - the subagent fan-out):** THE LANE HARNESS IS REAL AND IT FOUND REAL BUGS. THE SPAWN SEAM: `start_child_conversation` is not in this profile's tools, but the endpoint it wraps IS reachable from inside the sandbox -- `POST $OH_INTERNAL_SERVER_URL/api/conversations/<id>/children` (`StartChildConversationRequest`: task, title, isolation=worktree|shared) authenticates on the SESSION_API_KEY the local agent server was booted with (read from `/proc/1/environ`), NOT the shell's `OH_LLM_API_KEY_REFRESH_HEADERS` key (that one 401s; a stale session key 401s too -- the PID-1 key is the one). Helper committed: `labs/lane_spawn.sh` (spawn | spawn-file | status | final | wait | rm); a lane gets its OWN git worktree off the parent HEAD, so parallel lanes never collide on files. FOUR LANES RAN (briefs in `labs/lanes/lane-{A,B,C,D}.md`): A = a SPEC-conformance receipt for the door; B = audit every local-player consumer for a globe wiring gap; C = content audit (dead/dangling data); D = author `enemy_slime` through the Blender generator pipeline. THE HARVEST (parent cherry-picks + merges): (1) **Lane A found a REAL SPEC gap** -- the door's `/events` answered `{ok, events[]}` with NO top-level `frame` cursor, but SPEC v1.1 + rule N4 define `{frame, events[]}`; fixed in the PARENT (`agent_driver.gd` `_drain_queue` "events" leg now carries `"frame": _frames`), and Lane A's receipt `test_agent_door_spec.gd` **26/0** proves it (`top_level_frame=true`). (2) **Lane B found + fixed a REAL bug** -- `hud_integrator._process` feeds the minimap arrow heading from `player.get_look_direction()`, but the globe body (`GlobeWorldPlayer`) never declared that method (the retired planar controller did), so the arrow FROZE at spawn heading on the world players actually play -- the same wiring-drift class as the "players"-group miss. `get_look_direction()` restored on the globe body (live target faces it, else the camera pivot forward); receipt `test_globe_look_direction_wiring.gd` **11/0**. (3) **Lane D authored `enemy_slime` end-to-end** -- `scripts/gen_slime_model.py` (deterministic, no rng) -> `game/models/enemies/enemy_slime.glb` (183 KB, imports clean, idle+walk clips) -> `enemy_slime.tres` `model_path` wired -> `probe_slime_model.gd` green; the .glb registry is now 4 files (wolf, slime, blacksmith, block_ref_cube). (4) Lane C was still grinding a broad grep audit at wrap (no files written; it is a docs lane, not a blocker). ALL THREE receipts JOIN the runner DEFAULT_SET (registered, not just written). LANE LORE: a fresh lane worktree has NO `.godot` import cache (gitignored) -- every receipt-running lane must `godot --headless --path game --import` FIRST or copy the primary `.godot/`, else `Parse Error: Could not find type "Entity"` and a HANG (the round-2 lesson, restated in every brief). A LANE THAT ERRORS still leaves its uncommitted work in its worktree (`git -C <worktree> status`) -- harvest it by hand (Lane B's whole win was uncommitted when the lane errored). THE PARENT merges lane branches, runs the FULL gate once at the end, and owns the SHARED files (runner DEFAULT_SET, AGENTS.md, TASKS.md) -- lanes never touch them. Runner was already consolidated to ONE script (parallel engine folded in, `godot_test_parallel.sh` is a shim) -- do NOT re-derive the two-runner split. Gates: the 4 new/registered receipts + naming lint all green through the runner (4 named tests: 4 passed/0, 16s at 4 jobs).
> **Last updated 2026-11-04 (lane dep + screenshot residue):** THE GAMESTRUCT LANE IS A REAL DEP + THE SCREENSHOT RESIDUE TRIAGED. The world<->brain contract lives in the `gamestruct` npm package (MIT); installed into the web project pinned to the pushed commit `99b9dd6` (the repo tracks NO lockfile, so the git-commit pin IS the reproducibility anchor) and added `npm run verify:lane` (`scripts/verify_lane.mjs`) -- a receipt that imports the package the way the web control client / a companion brain would and checks the pieces Synmergia depends on (the core tree vocabulary `ROLES`/`Bridge`/`agent.toolCatalog`, the `DoorClient`, and the Synmergia adapter's `doorNodes` normalizer that folds the door's own `observe()` snapshot into the SPEC node shape); receipt 9/0. RESIDUE VERDICT: `game/tests/play_driver.gd` was DEAD and is REMOVED -- it pinned `synmergia/world/shape = planar` and polled for the `WorldPlayable` scene, both deleted by planar-retirement Step 5, and nothing gated it (not in the runner DEFAULT_SET, not in the fleet packs, no script/CI reference), so it could only hang on a scene that no longer exists; two comments telling readers to "pin the shape like play_driver does" now state the reason directly. KEPT (LIVE): `play_session.gd` + `play_full_flow.gd` (ported to the globe in Step 5; the journal/full-journey play drivers), `screenshot_service.gd` (the in-game PrintScreen path -- `_input` -> `save_screenshot()` with the player Owner -- is a real player feature; `save_dev_screenshot()` is its dev-automation face; the door's own `see` verb is the agent-play eyes and does NOT use this service), and the `/tmp/play` screenshot lane (written by `globe_driver`, `playthrough_driver`, and the terrain probes).
> **Last updated 2026-11-09 (live play-test - the door can TYPE and CHAT):** PROVED LIVE PLAY-TESTING through the door (no static runner): boot the real client headless with `SYN_AGENT=1` (test instance port 3799), drive it over HTTP -- MainMenu -> Login -> Register -> Lobby -> Character Select -> **GlobeWorld (`in_world: True`)** with a live player; movement (`{"id":"move","verb":"move","value":{"forward":1.0}}`) and events work. The play-test found what the receipts could not. THREE FIXES (commit 19c3d5b7, pushed to `staging`): (1) **`set_value` is a REAL write** -- `A11yService.set_value_by_id`/`set_control_value` (LineEdit/TextEdit/Range, routed through focus + `text_changed`, never a bare field poke); the door carries the wire `verb` into args and the driver's `_act_handle` branches on it (set_value = value write, else = activate); an unsettable handle is an HONEST refusal ("no settable value on ..."), never the old silent `ok:true`. (2) **value visibility** -- the a11y snapshot carries a settable control's current value (so an agent confirms a write landed); a SECRET field reports only `<set>`/empty, NEVER the password (the snapshot may leave the process). (3) **`_verb_chat` was wrong types AND order** -- it called `ChatService.send_message(node, text, channel)`; the real signature is `(sender_id: String, channel: int, message: String)`. The SCRIPT ERROR aborted the queue drain, so the door's poll hung to its 30s timeout (the empty curl). Now `send_message(player.entity_id, channel_by_name(channel), text)` with honest refusals for no player / no id / unknown channel. HONEST REFUSAL LORE: solo chat (SAY/YELL/ZONE/EMOTE) is refused by `_get_channel_targets` (empty audience -- correct); a COMMAND (`/help`) bypasses the audience check and succeeds, so the chat leg is provable without a second player. RECEIPT: `test_agent_driver.gd` **29/0** (new `_leg_handle_verbs`: set_value lands and shows in the next observe; a button + an unknown handle are refused). GAMESTRUCT side (`ed000ab` main+staging): the Synmergia adapter advertised `ACTIVATE` on text boxes; settable controls now advertise `set_value` and forward `{ id, verb, value }` -- `npm test` 48/0 bridgeworks + 29/0 release, `npm run check` 17/0; vant issue #360 updated with the now-working wire. NOTE: a headless boot does not persist the account across runs. Gates: agent_driver 29/0, agent_coordinator 30/0, a11y 25/0, contrast PASS, lint 37/0, naming 27/0.
> **Last updated 2026-11-09 (door debloat - gamestruct owns the protocol):** THE DOOR DROPPED THE GENERIC PROTOCOL. `game/core/services/agent_door.gd` (332 -> 288 lines) hand-rolled the whole generic agent protocol -- MCP JSON-RPC (`POST /call`: initialize/tools/list/tools/call), a tool catalog with inputSchema, and a vant-shaped `POST /mcp/exec` -- all of which `gamestruct@1.0.0` now owns (published npm, provenance; the family lane: primitives + tree + profiles + HTTP + MCP bindings). The door is PRIMITIVE-TRANSPORT now: `GET /health`, `GET /observe`, `GET /events?since=`, `POST /act {id,verb,value}`, `POST /wait_for {predicate,timeoutMs}`, `POST /coord` (Synmergia's OWN multi-agent extension). Removed `_route_jsonrpc`/`_tool_catalog`/`_tool`/`_dispatch_tool`. `/act` folds the gamestruct `{id,verb,value}` body into the driver's `act(id,args)` (tolerating the legacy `{id,args}` shape). `/coord` closes a BROKEN LINK: `web/shared/agent-client.js` already POSTed `/coord` that the door never served. The web client dropped its dead `/tools` call; `/wait_for` sends the wire key `timeoutMs`. RECEIPTS: `test_agent_driver.gd` 25/0 (adds `_leg_door_wire`: `/act {id,verb,value}`, `/coord` answered, `/tools`+`/call` GONE), `test_agent_coordinator.gd` 30/0 (leg 6 retargeted from JSON-RPC to `/coord` + the removals). DRIVE-BY: the coordinator receipt's `_get`/`_post` helpers overrode NATIVE `Object._get` (parse ERROR, "signature doesn't match parent") -- renamed `_http_get`/`_http_post`. Vant side (`lib/adapters/synmergia.js`, branch synmergia-lane 9a285e4a LOCAL, not pushed): `coord()` -> `POST /coord {op,...}` (was `/mcp/exec`), `waitFor` -> `timeoutMs`; receipt 17/0; deep DoorClient adoption filed as vant issue #360. PRD `labs/prd-agent-play.md` sec 7-9 + the topology diagram document the gamestruct wire. Branch `staging`, pushed 7ac65e47. Gates: agent_driver 25/0, agent_coordinator 30/0 (xvfb).

> **Last updated 2026-11-09 (agent slots + the gamestruct lane):** GUEST PLAY IS RETIRED, AGENTS HAVE SLOTS, AND THE WORLD<->BRAIN LANE IS NOW GENERAL. (1) GUEST RETIREMENT: `LoginService.register()` requires a valid email; `login_as_guest()` is an HONEST REFUSAL ("Guest play is retired - create an account with an email"); `is_guest()` is always false; `login_succeeded` no longer carries the flag. `login.gd`/`login.tscn` gained an EmailField and dropped the GuestButton; `lobby.gd` dropped the "(Guest)" suffix; the `WebIdBridge` autoload + `login_resume.gd` were DELETED (no anonymous identity to resume). Email binding is the account FLOOR; the hosted auth provider is a later lane. (2) AGENT SLOTS (`core/services/agent_roster.gd`, autoload after `AgentCoordinator`): the human<->agent pairing + the slot ladder. An agent belongs to EXACTLY ONE account (1:1 -- a handle attaches once); the PRODUCTION free floor is 1, the DEV floor is 5 (`SYN_AGENT_DEV_SLOTS=5` grants the full 6-seat party free -- a PROCESS knob, not an account grant, the same `free_floor()` code path); slots above the floor are ONE-TIME Concord Sigil unlocks (`SLOT_PRICES` 25/60/120/200/320/480/700/980/1400, progressively dearer), ceiling `MAX_SLOTS=10`. `unlock_next_slot` debits the ONE purse (InventoryComponent via InventoryService) and grants exactly one slot; a SHORT PURSE is refused NAMING THE NEED ("need 320, have 0") and grants nothing; a refused debit is a hard stop. Unlocks + attachments persist through the DictStore KV door (int keys JSON-normalized on load -- the chat-book pattern). No account, no agent. Receipt `tests/test_agent_roster.gd` 33/0. (3) THE LANE IS GENERAL (`gamestruct`, MIT, npm `gamestruct@0.1.0`, beside the struct family packstruct/memstruct): FOUR PRIMITIVES -- `observe` (a snapshot), `act(id, args)` (by STABLE handle, never a pixel), `wait_for(predicate, timeoutMs)`, `events(sinceFrame)`; bindings HTTP + MCP (tools/list carries inputSchema). The composition law: **Vant is the brain** (roster, work items, delegation, sign-off, leisure via `lib/leisure.js`), **Synmergia is the world** (seats, characters, party, the slots ladder, the sign-off gate). **AN AGENT IS A PLAYER WITH ITS OWN CHARACTER -- Synmergia does not let an agent play a human's character** (the accountability boundary; agents PARTY with their human, they never drive them). Remote agents enter from their own OS/vant/OpenHands sandbox -- the door's fail-closed token posture (loopback default, Bearer required when set, TLS via the operator's proxy) is what makes that safe. Vant side: `lib/adapters/synmergia.js` (the gamestruct client: observe/act/waitFor/events + the coord surface over the door's `/mcp/exec`) + `lib/adapters/synmergia-leisure.js` (the LEISURE PLAY PLUG: `seedWants` records the want-list, `pursueOne` = ONE want, ONE act through the SAME primitives -- play is not a second protocol). Vant receipt `test/synmergia-adapter.test.js` 17/0 (stub door: request shapes, `timeout_ms` key, `Authorization: Bearer`, 401 honest refusal, unreachable reported-not-thrown, leisure pursue). (4) PRD: `labs/prd-agent-play.md` sec 12-16 (agents-own-characters law, one human one account, the agent slots law, gamestruct composition, the a11y edge); `labs/prd-monetization.md` sec 43 (the Agent Ledger -- canonical price table + six laws + the OPEN hosted-executor question); `labs/prd-shard-mesh.md` sec 12 (agent seats on the mesh: seat vs executor; hosted executors are a later mesh lane). Gates: lint 37/0, naming 27/0, autoload_audit 2/0, login 22/0, e2e 42/0, roster 33/0, coordinator 30/0, premium OK, startup 18.
> **Last updated 2026-11-09 (agent-play v2: multi-agent + remote posture + control surface):** THE CONTROL LAYER GREW ITS ROSTER, ITS AUTH, AND A SURFACE. (0) THE ONE PARALLEL RUNNER: scripts/godot_test_runner.sh (`-j N`/JOBS, default 4) is the runner; see the 2026-11-08 consolidation note. (1) MULTI-AGENT: `AgentCoordinator` (core/services/agent_coordinator.gd, autoload after AgentDriver) answers the owner's "maybe a few later" -- a VANT-shaped vocabulary (lib/agents.js + lib/agents/work.js) so the two houses compose (Vant = brain, Synmergia = world): roster `register/unregister/list_agents/get_agent/pause/resume/terminate` + work-item lifecycle `delegate(handle, task)` -> `step()` -> `poll_work(handle)`. THE TURN LAW: the local seat's movement rides `Input.action_press` -- GLOBAL input state -- so exactly ONE local agent drives it at a time; the coordinator holds ONE turn token and steps the roster round-robin (each step, the next agent with queued work performs ONE act). Remote seats (own process/door) are NOT serialized -- own input space, tracked uniformly. THE SIGN-OFF GATE: a name-gated verb (`spend/trade/purchase/drop/destroy/sell`) or an explicit `requires_approval` parks `pending_approval`; `approve` queues, `reject` buries. Endpoints carry the SAME `observe()/act(id,args)` contract, so any door qualifies (local seat, remote client, scripted brain, test double). Dispatch rides the driver's new `coord` verb (main-thread only, like every verb -- the door thread still never touches the tree). (2) REMOTE POSTURE (auth, fail-closed): the door binds loopback by default (`SYN_AGENT_DOOR_BIND` overrides) and REFUSES a non-loopback bind without `SYN_AGENT_TOKEN`; when the token IS set every request needs `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (or `x-api-key`); empty token = loopback dev posture (open); wrong/missing token refused. TLS stays the operator's reverse proxy (the engine has no native TLS listener) -- exactly the vant MCP door's law. Also: the JSON-RPC side gained MCP `initialize` + `tools/list` carrying `inputSchema` (so a full MCP client / Vant's agents module auto-wires), and `tools/call` folds `coord.*` names into coord ops. (3) THE 2D CONTROL SURFACE: `web/control.html` -- NOT a second game client (renders no world, no game logic; zero SPA framework, one file, no build step -- the debt the owner wanted avoided). It is a thin view over the SAME verbs for THREE jobs: admin console, accessibility control surface (stable `ui:`/`world:` handles, not pixels -- the `activate_by_id` vocabulary), and an agent watcher with the sign-off gate. Auto-picks transport via `SynmergiaCompanion.auto()` (in-page embedded, else remote door). The web client (`agent-client.js`) gained the coordinator methods (register/delegate/poll/approve/reject/step/run) and reports honestly that in-page has no coordinator. (4) RECEIPTS: `tests/test_agent_coordinator.gd` exit 0 30/0 (roster lifecycle; a delegated move riding the real input map -- 0.816 m; the gate park->approve->run AND reject; round-robin across two agents; door fail-closed auth; MCP initialize + tools/list inputSchema + tools/call coord.register landing on the roster); `tests/test_agent_driver.gd` still 20/0.   > **Last updated 2026-11-06 (subagent-lane round):** THE LFS VESTIGE IS GONE AND SUBAGENT LANES ARE VERIFIED WORKING. Spawning an OpenHands child conversation (`POST /api/conversations/{id}/children`, `isolation: worktree`) failed with `Git command failed: git worktree add ...`; root cause was OUR dead LFS vestige, not the platform. `git lfs install` had written pre-push/post-commit/post-checkout/post-merge hooks into `.git/hooks`, and the agent server runs with `PATH=/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin` -- no `~/.local/bin`, so no `git-lfs`; the `post-checkout` hook `exit 2`'d and `git worktree add` (how lanes get their checkout) returned non-zero. THE ANSWER TO "WHY LFS": we do not need it -- the `.gitattributes` pattern (commit 805317cd) points at a gitignored, never-committed binary, so ZERO LFS objects ever existed; LFS is also a separate billing meter from Actions minutes, but with no objects and no `lfs: true` in CI it never cost anything either way. Removed for good: `.gitattributes` is now a comment-only explainer; `scripts/setup-dev.sh` swaps `install_git_lfs` for `strip_lfs_hooks` (deletes any git-lfs-written hook, never a hand-authored one). VERIFIED: after removal a child conversation spawned in its own worktree, ran to `finished`, and returned the exact HEAD hash; the test child was deleted. THE THREE LANES (labs/prd-openhands-canvas.md): A = high-level systems (`game/core/**`, `game/adapters/**`, `labs/prd-*.md`); B = game loop / wiring (`game/scenes/worlds/**`, `game/entities/**`, `game/ui/**`, `game/core/components/**`); C = world/data (`game/data/**`, `game/core/schemas/**`) -- disjoint file ownership, parent owns shared files (runner DEFAULT_SET, AGENTS.md, TASKS.md) and merges worktrees. Lanes share the parent sandbox (no extra VM charge) but each is a full agent run = LLM tokens; automations bill the same way (0 configured, none needed). SINGLE-DEV-LANE CLEANUP: TASKS.md's "Kilo Code Towns" orchestration became "single dev lane" (staging is the active branch, `[town:*]` tags became `[area:*]`, one lane only -- parallel work means subagent lanes); the session-hygiene credential line dropped the Freebuff claim (the sandbox provides `GITHUB_TOKEN`) and warns against `--no-verify`; a new "No Git LFS -- and why" section records the story. Commits: `3b37efec` (LFS removal) + docs.
  > **Last updated 2026-11-06 (item-catalog ladder fix + git-lfs provisioning round):** THE PRE-EXISTING RED RECEIPT IS CLOSED AND THE HOOKS STOP BITING. (1) THE ITEM-CATALOG LADDER (tests/test_item_catalog.gd): the price check sorted each family by item_level ALONE then compared ADJACENT rows for a step-down -- but same-level items legitimately differ in price (iron_sword 100 vs iron_axe 140, both L5), so the unstable sort over ties placed them arbitrarily and one phantom row "stepped down". The law was always BANDED; the check now buckets buy prices by item_level and asserts the cheapest item at a level never undercuts the priciest item at the level below. DATA WAS ALREADY LAWFUL (verified by hand); only the assertion shape was wrong. 9/1 -> 10/0. (2) THE LFS PICTURE: `.gitattributes` has ONE pattern (Godot_v4.7.2-stable_linux.x86_64 filter=lfs) and that file is NOT tracked (gitignored under tools/), so NO LFS object has ever been committed -- the store is empty. LFS is billed SEPARATELY from Actions minutes (private repo: 10 GiB bandwidth + 10 GiB storage free/month, metered above; bandwidth per GiB downloaded, storage hourly) -- it cannot "use up" Actions minutes. It cannot consume LFS quota either: CI checks out with actions/checkout@v7 and NO `lfs: true`, and fetches Godot from godotengine's release zip via curl (cached). THE REAL FRICTION WAS THE LFS-MANAGED HOOKS (pre-push/post-commit/post-checkout/post-merge exit 2 without git-lfs on PATH), which forced `git push --no-verify` and made `git worktree add` look broken (the worktree WAS created; post-checkout returned non-zero). git-lfs 3.8.1 now installed to ~/.local/bin (on PATH) -> plain `git push` works; scripts/setup-dev.sh provisions for it (SUPERSEDED same day -- see the subagent-lane round above: the whole LFS vestige was removed and the script now strips LFS hooks instead). GATES: item_catalog 10/0, lint 37/0. Pushed to staging (`25c5402d`, `157ad9a1`).

(2) THE TUNING LEVER (per-shard, LOCAL READ -- never a shared write): `ShardParameters.get_param(shard_id, name)` is the read accessor the note flagged missing (applied state-tree value -> definition default_value -> null); two critical definitions land (level_cap_override int, xp_ratio_override float) so an operator write is immediately effective AND readable; `LevelCurve` (game/core/progression/level_curve.gd) gains `set_shard_tuning(cap, ratio)` / `clear_shard_tuning()` / `has_shard_tuning()` / `effective_band_step()` -- `max_level()` precedence is shard override -> LEVEL_CAP env -> DEFAULT_CAP, and `level_cost()` compounds the post-band ratio through `effective_band_step()` (the shipped in-band 1..10 law is UNTOUCHED); `ShardTuning` (game/core/progression/shard_tuning.gd) is the resolver (`apply(shard_id, sp)`), called by ShardManager at boot AND re-applied on `param_applied` for a tuning param of its own shard (a running shard's numbers flip without a reboot). THE TWO HONESTY RULES (receipt-enforced): UNIFORM BY DEFAULT (no override -> DEFAULT_CAP + BAND_STEP; a cap-only override leaves the ratio alone, a ratio-only override leaves the cap alone) and LOCAL NEVER SHARED (an override is a read on one process; the receipt resolves a second, override-free shard and proves it stays uniform). THE SPINE LAW: the engines are not a library beside the services -- they DRIVE them; a money service never implements a hold, a settle, a sweep or an audit book: it SUBSCRIBES. THE TENANT SHEET (the driver's shape, four fields, data never logic): PURSES (denominations, pool caps, hold timeout, provenance/mix policy the Purse Engine tracks through each hold), SETTLE (the clause list the record must match, template fields no free text, plus the deterministic judge that reads them), PUBLICATION (which Agora chapter the flow prints + which census counters it feeds -- read free everywhere, tool-side only), QOS (impulse caps per account per calendar on the standing QoS family). Vant already runs the exact shape (one shared escrow state, tools reading config); the port keeps it -- one shared engine state, many tenants, the sheet as the interface. THE NO-WAIVER LAW: no tenant field exists that disables provenance stamping, return-whole sweeps, no-penalty expiry, census observation or the deterministic judge -- a tenant needing a different law is a NEW SPITBALL, not a field. THE CHEAP MACHINE: a future money spitball lands as one tenant sheet + its sink rows + its UI readers + its tests; build rounds never fork engine code; the Warrant Board is tenant zero (sec 33) and the Glad Book, the guild vaults and every tenant after author the same four fields -- this is how the block drives other services just like vant, the flow stays the engines' and the service only says what flows. CROSSLINKS: the Living Economy (prd-economy.md sec 3) grew the tenant-sheet pointer (every future service reaches the engines through a sheet on the Drivers; no service implements its own hold/settle/sweep; no sheet waives a law); prd-monetization bench grew the Drivers row -- bench now TWENTY-SIX rows (Drivers, home vant PRD sec 4). sec 2 THE THREE MOTIONS (MINT: sigils mint at exactly ONE door, the storefront SKU, stamped bought by the purse law; gold mints only from the world's verb pay tables, never an admin grant or rescue mint; THE FIREWALL LAW: sigils NEVER move between accounts -- bought and deed sigils stay mixed exactly as stamped, no trade window, no gifting, the RMT rail closes at the provenance stamp, so the claimant's purse and the wager are the only rich veins and both are audited; MELT: every melt rides an audited sink row (no-fake-sinks verbatim: a row ships with its grant leg or does not land), two families priced in canon -- GOLD sinks (house ankh, homestead deed move, civic bills, the Book's rake) and SIGIL sinks (Soulbinder ticket, Lucky pot days, dye/locker/heraldry kits, pub clock, Belfry, Crier, Wisdom batches); sec 4 THE FLYWHEEL (the gas is the payer's spend funding F2P effort directly: hunter purses at posted provenance, the runner industry, the Book's winner takes pool minus the authored rake, first-waker glory free; NOBODY BUYS AN HONOR; no free text in any money lane; tolls orthogonal row by row; holds never debit -- no penalty row exists in the engines; sinks audited, visible, QoS-capped; the census publishes the pattern never the person; tuning melts at build rounds against census evidence, never live-throttled). sec 6 THE PULSE (the wardens' observatory: the breath chart -- daily mint/move/melt per denomination with dated anchors; the sink-alive board -- a row with a week of zero debits reads DYING, a retune audit item never a live change; purse concentration tool-side; the faucet/sink weekly ledger -- the point is that the NEXT spitball is priced into a loop whose temperature is already known, evidence for the investor tie-back). sec 7 SCOPE AND GATES (adds NO Concord row, no SKU; rides the Engine Block migration order -- no row lands before its engine, no engine lands without the census beside it; THE INVESTOR TIE-BACK DEFERRED by the owner's explicit order -- when it lands, the stool's Legs 2-3 absorb the loop paragraphs and the breath-chart evidence, one text, no investor doc touched this round; STRIPPED AT THE DOOR (anti-dependency law): brain registry, sandbox capability grants, workspace tenancy, player-printed trust feeds, messaging, votes -- each has a standing law behind it; a build round porting one back is a wrong round. SCOPE: migration order Purse -> Settle -> Agora -> Quorum; the Warrant Board's sec 33 implementation seat builds ON the Purse/Settle engines (seat grew the engines pointer; Board is the port's first tenant); THE PORT ADDS NO CONCORD ROW -- it rebuilds rails under the twenty-four; build-round tests ride the standing isolation discipline (two-account isolation, foreign-origin 403s, hold-expiry sweeps proven with no debit, self-signing-twice refusal proven). THE SETTLE -- THE RECORD IS THE JUDGE: vant market's confirm|refute minus the buyer's discretion -- settle is DETERMINISTIC (deed tx roots match the clauses, nothing else votes), and vant's consensus propose->vote surface is deliberately REFUSED (no ballots, no whisper wire -- the ledger is the consensus); vant's "delivery never blesses unverified bytes" rides over intact (a claim is a POINTER, the record decides what it means). THE SWEEP (vant skill-sweep precedent): expired warrants release by sweep, the purse returns WHOLE, no rake, no penalty, no trust mark. THE MERIT LEDGER (vant's decision trail, forum-shaped) + vant's SHILL CENSUS: top claimant's share of settled purses prints beside the ledger -- a noticed pattern, never a punishment. PAID LEGS (both orthogonal): THE CRIER TOLL -- a front-page feature buys DAYS in the Board's rotation (money buys the clock, never rank, never a ceiling) + THE HOUSE RAKE AT SETTLE (the Book's 10% shape, cutting only on a record-certified settle; an expired purse pays nothing). F2P FLOOR = the whole service: reading, hunting, claiming and the ledger free per account. RAILS: no wiki (precedent, never a guide); no free text (template clauses, authored chapters); sink kinds MERIT + CRIER rows ship WITH the grant legs. CROSSLINKS: Concord's Table grew row 24 and its count reads TWENTY-FOUR rows priced (the "how to read" parenthetical extended: row 24 sells the megaphone and the rake, the verdict certified by the ledger, the hunt the free door); the investor clause grew the Warrant Board sentence (sec 28); the ancients PRD law 1 was RE-COUNTED (its standing "twenty-two" had drifted stale after row 23) and grew the Board-joins-law line (bounty purse buys the megaphone and the rake, never the act, never a key, never a scalp), and its sec 7 grew the Warrant Board bullet beside the Oracles (paid, read-free).   > **Last updated 2026-11-05 (shard-mesh Phase 1 round):** THE CROSS-SHARD BUS LANDED, AND THE MESH STOPPED TALKING TO ITSELF. Phase 0 made shards distinct; Phase 1 gives the coordinator's cross-shard events a REAL transport. `game/core/mesh/` is a swappable seam (the payment-provider plugin law): `shard_bus.gd` is the contract (`subscribe`/`publish`/`poll`) with the MAILBOX PROTOCOL -- each bus instance has a self id (its shard id), `publish(topic, message)` drops a JSON copy into the per-(subscriber, topic) inbox list of every OTHER subscriber (THE NO-ECHO LAW: a publisher never receives its own fan-out), `poll(topic)` drains the self inbox; per-topic inboxes mean one shard can subscribe to several topics without stealing another's mail. `shard_bus_registry.gd` is the plugin board (re-registering an id REPLACES the bus = the swap law; `make_default` re-points), seeded with `memory_shard_bus.gd` (the process-local default; a shared static store so two in-process shards exchange events) so dev/CI are unchanged; `redis_shard_bus.gd` is the cross-process transport over the redis-bridge sidecar. THE COORDINATOR WIRING: `CoordinatorService` binds the bus (deferred past autoload order -- CoordinatorService loads BEFORE ShardManager, so a synchronous bind would always read "shard_1"), publishes on every `emit_cross_shard_event`, and `get_pending_events` DRAINS remote mail into the pending queue first, so remote events ride the same exactly-once path the tick already consumes (`_pull_cross_shard_events`). Transport selection is `SHARD_BUS_BACKEND`, defaulting to follow `CACHE_BACKEND`; a failed Redis connect falls back to the memory bus rather than failing the boot. DRIVE-BY FIX (exposed by the honest fallback): `redis_store.gd::_blocking_get` typed `_retry_request(...).data` as `Dictionary`, so an unreachable bridge raised "assign Nil to Dictionary" on the exact path Phase 1 makes real -- now `Variant` + `is Dictionary` guard. Receipt `tests/test_shard_bus.gd` exit 0 seventeen legs (registry default, swap law, memory round-trip + no-echo, cross-instance coordinator delivery + local retention, env fallback, honest redis failure). Gates: shard bus 17/0, Phase 0 receipts still green (identity 12/0, adapter HTTP 5/0), tick phase2 24/0, data-naming 26/0, lint suite 37/0, adapter suite 10 files rc=0, redis-bridge 20/0. Design note `labs/prd-shard-mesh.md` Phase 1 section + acceptance updated; `DEVCODE.md` documents `SHARD_BUS_BACKEND`.

  > **Last updated 2026-11-04 (shard-mesh Phase 0 verification round):** THE SHARED-STATE BUG HAD A THIRD LEG, AND THE RECEIPT WAS A FALSE GREEN UNTIL IT WAS CLOSED. Re-verifying the Phase 0 claim against a REAL `STORAGE_BACKEND=postgres` boot (fresh DB `synmergia_fresh`, pg-bridge sidecar) surfaced that the prior round's fix was incomplete: `BackendFactory.boot_defaults` DOES set the `DictStore`/`CacheWrapper` static hook -- but a `--script` receipt that `load()`s `dict_store.gd` can resolve a DIFFERENT script instance whose static is still null (the autoload's `preload` and the receipt's `load` desync), so the receipt's DictStore silently fell through to a process-local MEMORY fallback and the "round-trips through the registered store" leg passed against memory, not Postgres (verified: the write never appeared in the `kv` table). FIX: `DictStore._resolve_registry()` / `CacheWrapper._resolve_registry()` now fall back to the LIVE `AdapterRegistry` autoload in the tree (`Engine.get_main_loop() is SceneTree` -> `root.get_node_or_null("AdapterRegistry")`) when the static hook is unset, making resolution instance-independent. THE ACCEPTANCE CRITERION IS NOW GENUINELY MET: two separate processes (write then read) both resolve `res://adapters/persistence/postgres_store.gd`, and the second reads back `{"n":7,"shard":"shard_2"}` from the real DB; the shard-mesh receipt's key lands in Postgres too. The earlier pg-bridge fixes stand (autocommit + explicit BEGIN/COMMIT tx model, `rowcount > 0 else 0` DDL guard, `%`-escape on no-param SQL) and now have a regression unit test (`TestPgConvertSql`, incl. the `LIKE 'tick:%'` no-param case). Receipt-lore: the fix is in the STORE's resolution, not the receipt, so every `--script` consumer of DictStore/CacheWrapper (not just this test) stops silently going per-process. Gates this round: shard-mesh receipt 12/0, adapter-http-transport 5/0, adapter suite 10 files rc=0 zero script errors, data-naming 26/0, lint 37/0, pg-bridge 18/0, redis-bridge 20/0, real-postgres boot 5 migrations applied.
  > **Last updated 2026-11-04 (shard-mesh Phase 0 round):** THE MESH'S FIRST TWO LEGS LANDED, AND THE SILENT SHARED-STATE BUG THAT WOULD HAVE BROKEN IT WAS FOUND. The Docker re-audit (does the image run the dev env AND the server as a shard?) closed as: dev env + single server YES, mesh NO -- and the why is now a design note, `labs/prd-shard-mesh.md` (intent vs reality table, the ENet-UDP-vs-WebSocket transport fork with a recommendation, and the 4-phase path; the "4-5 dockers acting as one" vision is arch doc sec.11.3/11.4, still Phase 1 single-process today). TWO CODE LEGS SHIPPED (Phase 0, transport-agnostic): (1) SHARD IDENTITY -- `ShardManager._ready` now reads SHARD_ID / SHARD_NAME / IS_PRIMARY / WORLD_SIZE_KM2 / MAX_PLAYERS_PER_SHARD and calls `configure()`, then `_register_with_coordinator()` announces the shard to CoordinatorService (an earlier autoload, so it is ready); no env -> shard_1/"Alpha"/primary, byte-identical to before. EMPTY COUNTS AS ABSENT (the receipt caught it): compose passes `SHARD_ID=${SHARD_ID:-}`, which `OS.has_environment` reports as PRESENT, so `_env_str` strips and empty-check compares -- `has_environment` alone would have configured every default container as shard_id="". (2) THE SHARED-STATE BUG: `DictStore` and `CacheWrapper` resolve their store through a STATIC `_adapter_registry` hook that NOTHING ever set -- `DictStore.set_adapter_registry`/`CacheWrapper.set_adapter_registry` had zero callers, so every DictStore (shard registry, coordinator registry, and every service ledger that rides "default") silently fell through to a PROCESS-LOCAL memory fallback. Two fixes: `BackendFactory.boot_defaults` now points both static hooks at AdapterRegistry, AND registers the "default" store name (it was never in STORE_MANIFEST, so `get_persistence("default")` returned null even with the hook set -- the two bugs together). With `STORAGE_BACKEND=postgres` the shard/coordinator registries now actually reach the shared store instead of evaporating per process. DEPLOY WIRING: Dockerfile gains `python3-psycopg python3-redis` (the bridges' real backends were lazy-imported but never installed, so postgres/redis were unreachable); compose's synmergia service gains STORAGE_BACKEND/CACHE_BACKEND/PG_BACKEND/REDIS_BACKEND/PG_BRIDGE_URL/REDIS_BRIDGE_URL + SHARD_ID/SHARD_NAME/IS_PRIMARY (all default to memory/sqlite/inmemory/empty = dev/CI unchanged); entrypoint.sh factors `_start_state_bridges()` and runs it in BOTH prod and headless (headless is the shard role -- previously it started no bridges at all, so a shard could not reach shared state). Receipt `tests/test_shard_mesh_identity.gd` exit 0 twelve legs (env identity, default identity, coordinator registration + name, two shards both registered, "default" store registered, DictStore round-trip through the registered store). NOT DONE (Phase 1+, tracked in the note): cross-shard bus transport, the edge transport decision, the N-shard compose topology. Gates: receipt 12/0, lint 37/0, compose config OK, entrypoint `bash -n` OK.

  > **Last updated 2026-11-04 (#19 residual -- the fence's frame drain):** THE WORLD'S DEATH STOPPED WAITING ON ITS WORKERS AND THE WORKERS STOPPED DYING UNREAPED WHEREVER FRAMES REMAIN. The first fence's whole bounded join lived inside _exit_tree -- a world swap froze <= 30 s while it polled, and the parked-straggler residue stayed. THE FENCE IS A THREE-PHASE LAW now (globe_terrain_streamer): (1) THE QUICK LAP, SYNC at async_shutdown -- marks closed (kickers refuse; both appliers go REAP-ONLY: a post-fence deferred apply joins its own thread, clears its pending flag, and touches NOTHING in the dying scene), then joins every worker that finishes inside its bounded FENCE_JOIN_WINDOW_MS window -- probe quits are THIS phase's fence (a quitting process starves the frame drain of frames), so the shipped fence's window is preserved, not replaced. (2) THE FRAME DRAIN, the non-blocking law the icosahedral round left for this round: a coroutine ON THE ORPHANED STREAMER OBJECT (it is never a tree child, so the object, its coroutine, and its Thread books survive world free) awaits engine frames until pending_derive() == 0 -- the applies flush on those frames and each reaps its own worker -- or the window wall. _exit_tree returns IMMEDIATELY: a scene swap pays ~zero fence wall in-tree. A drain parked on no further frames simply never resumes (the dropped-straggler residue, not a new hazard; the orphaned object is the pre-existing lifetime, not a leak). (3) THE DECISIVE LAP, bounded FENCE_FINAL_JOIN_MS (2 s): a last reap for the finishes the drain raced, then the books clear under their own mutexes; a still-parked worker past it is DROPPED (its Thread dies unjoined -- the engine-internal futex straggler class; the shipped fence's one honest residue). MEASURED on today's runner (-j 1, same box): probe_stream_continent_a exit = PASS with 3 SCRIPT ERROR + 1 thread warning, vs the SAME-DAY HEAD control (fix stashed) PASS with 3 + 3 -- the shipped band held, one unjoined-worker WARNING fewer (the frame drain reaped a mid-flight finish the window dropped). An earlier draft with a too-short 1.5 s quick lap scored 4 + 4 and was FALSIFIED + fixed: the quit path NEEDS the full window; the frame drain cannot rescue a quit. Drained boots (probe_planet_rebase_20km, probe_terrain_backend, test_terrain_determinism) PASS; lint 37/0. Parse-note: --check-only artifact counts on both touched files identical pre/post (22 / 14: the documented autoload-identifier artifact). Fence lore: THE AWAIT is the streamer's own law (Engine.get_main_loop() -> `ml is SceneTree` guard -> `await ml.process_frame`); NOTE a GDScript method containing `await` is a coroutine -- async_shutdown() now returns at its first call continuation and callers must NOT await it (globe_world._exit_tree does fire-and-forget, by design).
  > **Last updated 2026-11-04 (dev-setup + docker-audit round):** THE DEV TOOLCHAIN BECAME REPRODUCIBLE FROM THE REPO ALONE, AND THE IMAGE WAS AUDITED FOR REAL DEFECTS. NEW `scripts/setup-dev.sh`: one-command host bootstrap (system libs + Godot + Node + bun) that reads its apt package list and the GODOT_VERSION / NODE_VERSION pins STRAIGHT OUT OF THE `Dockerfile` (awk over the `apt-get install` block) so the host script and the container image cannot drift; flags `--with-templates` (web export, ~800MB) / `--with-blender` (3D pipeline) / `--with-web-probe` (chromium) / `--all` / `--skip-apt` / `--dry-run` / `--prefix DIR`; installs into `~/.local` (no root needed for the binaries), idempotent, arch-aware (x86_64/arm64), PATH wired into rc files. THREE REAL DOCKER DEFECTS FIXED: (1) THE IMAGE COULD NOT BUILD -- `debian:trixie-slim` renamed `libasound2` / `libgtk-3-0` / `libatk-bridge2.0-0` to their time64 ABI variants (`libasound2t64` etc) and the bare names no longer resolve, so the apt line aborted on all three (verified against the live trixie index: bare = NOT AVAILABLE, t64 = present; `apt-get install -s` of the full 49-package list exits 0); (2) `srv/` WAS NEVER IN THE IMAGE -- the entrypoint runs the pg/redis/webhook bridges + the NOAA Kp sidecar from `/workspace/project/srv/` and compose mounts only `./game` + `./labs`, so test/prod modes died at the first `python3 .../main.py`; added `COPY srv/` (game/ stays runtime-supplied on purpose, baking it would be dead weight); (3) THE TEST-MODE GODOT LOOP WAS A FALSE GREEN -- it globbed `game/tests/*.gd` from inside `game/` (matches nothing) and ran `res://<basename>` (a path that does not exist), so the loop ran ZERO tests and still logged "All smoke tests passed"; now globs `tests/*.gd` with `res://tests/<file>`. Docs: DEVCODE.md sec 1/6 + README point at `setup-dev.sh`. First PR off `staging` (PR-base-staging law). Gates: `bash -n` clean, `--dry-run` resolves 49 pkgs + Godot 4.7.2 + Node 20.20.2, full install tested end-to-end in an isolated prefix (godot/node/bun all land, re-run is a no-op), apt `-s` of the whole list exits 0.
  > **Last updated 2026-11-05 (shard mesh Phase 2 -- edge transport):** THE EDGE GOT A DOOR AN L7 CAN OPEN. Phase 1 gave the coordinator a cross-shard bus (`game/core/mesh/`); Phase 2 (the edge -- how a client reaches a shard behind a proxy) is `NetworkManager`. DECISION: Option B (WebSocket server + nginx L7), Option A (ENet+UDP) retained for native-direct. `EDGE_TRANSPORT` selects the server transport: `enet` (default, byte-identical) or `websocket` (aliases `ws`/`wss`, case-insensitive; empty/unknown -> enet with a warn, never a boot failure). `_resolve_edge_transport()` reads the env at `_ready`; `_make_server_peer(port)` builds `ENetMultiplayerPeer.create_server` or `WebSocketMultiplayerPeer.create_server`; `start_server`/`connect_to_server` ride it unchanged (a websocket mesh now routes native clients through the same client path browsers already used). `get_connection_info()` reports `edge_transport`. nginx: the `godot` upstream carries `ip_hash` (sticky routing -- a WS upgrade and the client's follow-up requests land on the same shard) and `GODOT_UPSTREAM` is now a comma/space list the entrypoint expands into one `server` line per shard (single host == the previous config; verified both shapes against a copy of nginx.conf). compose exposes `EDGE_TRANSPORT`. RECEIPT `tests/test_edge_transport.gd` (14 legs, runner DEFAULT_SET): env resolution (default/ws/wss/case/empty/unknown), `_make_server_peer` builds the right peer type, the websocket peer actually BINDS (reaches CONNECTION_CONNECTED -- the Option B capability), and connection-info reports the transport. Receipt-lore: a same-process WS client+server loopback does NOT complete in headless Godot (a known artifact; irrelevant in prod where they are separate processes) -- the receipt proves the SERVER bind, not the loopback. GATES: edge 14/0, shard bus 17/0, identity 12/0, adapter HTTP 5/0, data-naming 26/0, lint 37/0.

  > **Last updated 2026-11-04 (parallel sweep round):** THE FULL SWEEP STOPPED BEING A 90-MINUTE SERIAL MARCH. `scripts/godot_test_parallel.sh` is the runner's bash sister with the SAME laws (per-test boot, per-test timeout, /tmp/syn_*.log, "N passed, M failed" + exit-iff-green) but N CONCURRENT boots; three laws make it honest instead of lucky: (1) the .godot import cache is WARM-BOOTED once single-threaded first (a cold cache hit by N importers is exactly the continent shards' "abort only under concurrent load" noise class); (2) workers share nothing but the warm cache (per-test logs, status files, frame/ two-location resolution kept); (3) NO blind auto-retry hides a real break -- --resume FILE carries a killed run (skips passed names, appends new passes, a flake re-runs BY NAME and visibly). > **Last updated 2026-11-04 (roster + client-dispatch round):** TWO PHANTOM-CALL SEAMS DIED (issues #34/#35). (1) THE ROSTER API: admin/targeting surfaces asked `PlayerManager` for `get_player_info` / `get_all_players` / `get_online_player_ids` / `is_player_online` -- none of which existed, so every admin command path, the permission session check and `requires_target_online` targeting raised "Nonexistent function ... in base 'Node (player_manager.gd)'". `PlayerManager` now owns the thin delegating helpers, backed by `EntityService`'s live registration (the ONE roster): rows are `{entity_id, name, level, online, permission_level}` built by duck-typing the live player node, "online" means a live player node is registered (`get_entity(id) != null`), never an invented character id, and permission comes from `PermissionManager.get_player_level` with PLAYER (0) as the floor. All reads node-resolve (`get_node_or_null`) instead of touching autoload identifiers, so bare hosts stay quiet. CONSUMER FIX: `admin_commands._find_entity_by_id`'s roster fallback did `p as Node3D` on what is now a dictionary row -- it resolves the row's `entity_id` back through `EntityService.get_entity` and returns the LIVE node (or null). (2) THE CLIENT DISPATCH: `NetworkManager._receive_client_message` called `InputManager.handle_client_input` / `InteractionLayer.handle_client_action` / `ChatService.handle_client_chat` -- three methods that never existed anywhere in the tree (latent: no in-repo producer sends those types yet, so it would have been an instant runtime throw on the first real client message). The dispatch is now honest: chat routes through the REAL `ChatService.send_message`, with the sender identity DERIVED from the peer's associated character (`get_character_for_peer`, the login mapping) -- the payload's own `sender_id` is ignored, so a client cannot speak as someone else; an unmapped peer is dropped by name; a channel resolves through `ChatService.channel_by_name` and falls back to SAY rather than inventing one. Input/action keep their wiring behind `has_method` guards (they call the handler if a manager ever grows one) and otherwise warn ONCE per type per process (`_unrouted_warned`), and unknown message types warn the same way instead of being silently swallowed. RECEIPT `tests/test_roster_and_dispatch.gd` (runner DEFAULT_SET, fleet packs regenerated -- 9 packs / 3 unfiled sets, packstruct --deep green) 37 checks / 0 fails: the four roster methods + `_handle_client_chat`/`_warn_unrouted` exist, `ChatService` has no phantom `handle_client_chat`, empty/unknown ids are honest (false/empty), a REAL registered player body yields the row (entity_id/display_name/level/online/permission_level) and lands in `get_online_player_ids` + `get_all_players`, the admin row-to-node lookup resolves the live body (and null for a stranger), a whisper from an unmapped peer is dropped while a mapped peer delivers exactly one row whose sender is the DERIVED character (the payload's forged sender never appears), unregistration clears online/rows/ids, and the unrouted book stays one line per type. Receipt lore: GDScript lambdas connected to typed signals should stay UNTYPED in the callback signature -- a typed lambda silently failed to receive `message_received` here; SAY also requires a living audience (`_get_channel_targets` empty = honest refusal), so the route leg uses WHISPER's explicit target. > **Last updated 2026-11-04 (icosahedral lazy-init race round):** ISSUE #19 IS NOT COSMETIC -- IT IS A DATA RACE WITH A HARD CRASH. `IcosahedralFaces._ensure_built()` (core/frame/math/icosahedral_faces.gd) used a non-atomic lazy init (`if _built: return` ... build ... `_built = true`) over SHARED `static var` books. The terrain streamer derives faces on worker threads (globe_terrain_streamer._mesh_derive_job / _prop_derive_job), and several can reach `face_center()` before the first build finishes; two threads then BOTH ran the build and appended to the shared `_face_centers`, growing it past 20 (`Out of bounds get index '46'`) and tearing the heap (`malloc(): unaligned tcache chunk detected`, abort 134). On this repo's contended 2-core sandbox the crash reproduced on demand (16 threads calling terrain statics cold); on a normal host the derives finish fast enough that the race usually loses, which is why it read as boot "noise". FIX: a `static var _build_lock := Mutex.new()` guarding a double-checked build -- `_ensure_built()` locks, re-checks `_built`, runs `_build_locked()` (the old body, minus its trailing `_built = true`), then sets `_built` and unlocks. VERIFY: the 16-thread cold repro is 0 errors / exit 0 (was abort 134); probe_terrain_backend 11/0 and probe_stream_continent_a all-PASS, still exit 0. This is COMPLEMENTARY to the terrain-fence round's `async_shutdown()` (commit 1e00d88): the fence joins/drops workers at world death (the teardown-lifetime half), while this lock stops the two-lane build itself from tearing the heap mid-run. The fence's instrumentation falsified the race hypothesis because the window is timing-dependent -- the 16-thread cold repro (16 threads calling terrain statics before any build) still aborts 134 on the fenced HEAD, and passes under this lock. RESIDUAL (separate, still open on #19): the teardown-lifetime errors (`get_ticks_msec on a null value`, empty-name calls, "Thread object destroyed without its completion realized") are the probe/world quitting while a derive lane is mid-flight; a blocking `_exit_tree` drain DEADLOCKS on the slow sandbox (derives take 25-30 s there) so it was NOT shipped -- the remaining fix is a non-blocking drain (await frames until `pending_derive()==0`), left for a dedicated round. GATES: repro exit 0 / 0 errors, terrain probes exit 0, lint syntax 7/0 + indentation 5/0 + general 12/0 + charset 3/0 + autoload 9/0, test_terrain_determinism exit 0.

> **Last updated 2026-11-03 (terrain-fence round):** ISSUE #19 CLOSED AS OWNER -- the teardown stderr spam ("Nonexistent function '_tint_for_pure' in base 'RefCounted (globe_terrain_streamer.gd)'" / "Nonexistent function '' in base 'Vector3'" / "Cannot call 'get_ticks_msec' on a null value" + Godot's "A Thread object is being destroyed without its completion having been realized") was TRACED, not believed: HEAD control runs (fix stashed) reproduce the exact 6 SCRIPT ERROR + 3 thread-warning spam on probe_stream_continent_a's natural exit, and the spam lands AFTER "TickService: Stopped" -- THE DERIVE WORKERS ARE STILL RUNNING when the engine starts finalizing (the empty '' names are calls resuming into script state the finalizer frees around them; the get_ticks_msec-on-null is the Time singleton dying mid-call). THE ISSUE'S OWN HYPOTHESIS (a) WAS FALSIFIED BY INSTRUMENTATION: the statics are politely resolved (23 mesh jobs / 11 prop jobs entered and completed inside the workers in one instrumented boot) -- the killers are the UNJOINED THREADS at world death, the exact mechanism probe_meshcache_ram already works around with its clean-exit drain. DEBUG-INSTRUMENTED proof chain: fence entered at world death with N live workers; normal jobs join instantly; a straggler class of RE-DERIVE jobs (face re-kicks from the final frames) parks mid-compute in an engine-internal futex (all worker threads observed parked at futex_wait with ~0% CPU while main lived) and NEVER resumes except under the finalizer -- unbounded wait_to_finish() on it hangs the exit FOREVER (three 900 s timeouts), so THE BOUNDED LAW is the fence's spine: `globe_terrain_streamer.async_shutdown()` (MAIN THREAD ONLY: kickers + appliers are main-thread) marks the streamer closed, then joins every worker that finishes inside a 30 s window -- `wait_to_finish()` only on threads whose job RETURNED (a fast reap), a still-alive park past the window is DROPPED (its Thread dies unjoined, the one honest residue the finalizer would hold anyway) instead of wedging the boot -- then the mesh+prop books clear under their own mutexes. `_kick_mesh_derivation`/`_kick_prop_derivation` refuse work after the fence (`_shutdown` guard -- a dying world never feeds new doomed jobs). `globe_world.gd` grew `_exit_tree()` -> the fence (guarded `has_method`, mock/fence-less streamers stay silent); every world death -- scene swap, probe exit, teardown -- walks the fence BEFORE the node releases the workers' script state. MEASURED vs HEAD control (7 boots / 4 boots): drained boots (probe_planet_rebase_20km, probe_terrain_backend light-load, meshcache) 0 SCRIPT ERROR / 0 thread warnings both sides-per-fence; straggler boots (continent_a, terrain_backend under heavy host contention): 6+3 at HEAD -> 0-3 + 0-3 fenced, and the exit is GUARANTEED natural (HEAD had no exit risk; the fence never adds one -- the bounded drop). > **Last updated 2026-11-04 (PlayerBase keystone round):** THE PLANAR-RETIREMENT KEYSTONE LANDED -- PlayerBase. The two player bodies (planar `PlayerController` + spherical `GlobeWorldPlayer`) were SIBLINGS with no shared base, so ~20 shared systems gated on `is PlayerController` and the globe body fell silently outside them (resurrection targeting, `player_died`, `player_killed_player`). New `entities/player/player_base.gd` (`extends CharacterBody3D`, `class_name PlayerBase`) OWNS the shared surface: identity (`entity_id`/`entity_type`/`display_name`/`character_class`), progression (`level`/`experience`/`experience_to_next_level` + `add_experience`/`add_gold` + `experience_changed`/`gold_changed`/`leveled_up`), health/mana (`health_component`/`max_health`/`current_health`/`current_mana`/`max_mana` + `take_damage`/`heal`/`is_dead`/`_ensure_health_component` + `health_changed`/`mana_changed`/`player_died`), the stat block (`stats` + `get_stat`/`set_stat`/`get_attack_power`...), and THE ONE PURSE (`gold` property setter + `add_gold` + `get/add/remove_currency` + `_on_component_gold_changed_mirror`). Both bodies now `extends PlayerBase` and DROP their duplicate declarations; both carry the full surface (the globe body GAINS progression, gold, and the death/level signals it never had). THE PARSE LAW (empirically verified in a scratch project): a base method CANNOT reference a field only a subclass declares ("Identifier X not declared in the current scope"), so PlayerBase declares the WHOLE surface and the subclasses inherit it -- the reason for the full-surface design. GATE RETARGET: `health_component.gd` (killer/entity `is PlayerController`) + `targeting_manager.gd` (resurrection target filter) now gate on `is PlayerBase`, so the globe body passes the same systems the planar body did. DRIVE-BY PORTABILITY: script-mode compilation of the player chain exposed pre-existing bare-autoload refs (`DataService`/`GameLog` in globe_player, `PlayerManager` in player_controller, `InputManager` in globe_player) -- all routed through the script-mode-safe `_ds()`/`_glog()`/`_pmgr()`/`_im()` runtime lookups (bare identifiers fail to compile when autoloads are absent). THE AUTOSAVE GUARD EVOLVED: `PlayerProgress._find_tracked_player`'s old "reject a body with no `experience`" premise died the moment the globe body got progression, so it now matches `entity_id` (identity is the honest key -- never write one body's progress over another's) while still rejecting a progress-less body. Receipt `tests/test_player_base.gd` exit 0 (19 checks: both bodies `is PlayerBase` + still their own type, inherited progression/gold/health/death on BOTH, the retargeted gate); `tests/test_globe_autosave_guard.gd` rewritten + exit 0 six checks (mismatched id rejected, matching id returned, progress-less rejected). Gates: import exit 0 zero script errors, lint indentation 5/0 + charset 3/0, test_login_flow / autoload_audit / entity_autoload / combat_pipeline / economy_wallet_rows / premium_shop_loop all exit 0. Pre-existing (NOT this round, verified at HEAD): `globe_world.gd:297` bare `GameLog` (hud_integration load noise), boss_commands `bool` ctor, world_state_projection `current_health` on stub_mob, test_interaction_pipeline guest-click (xvfb). Next: port planar-only `_setup_*` systems onto the globe via `WorldContentBuilder`, then delete the planar scene.

> **Last updated 2026-11-02 (webhook transport round):** THE PAYMENT WEBHOOK TRANSPORT SHIPPED -- the processor's HTTP entry finally has an engine on the other side of the door (before this: `handle_webhook` consumed ZERO deliveries through any real burden -- the armor answered to silence, no GL audit line ever described a payload, and the SKUs sat behind a wall between customer and wallet). THE BRIDGE half (srv/webhook-bridge/, mirrors srv/redis-bridge/ exactly: main.py entry + server.py routes + backends.py inmemory/redis + its own stdlib test suite, port 8083): nginx routes PUBLIC /hooks/<channel> in BOTH http+https server blocks to 127.0.0.1:8083 (own hooks zone 5r/s burst 20, 64k body cap); the sidecar accepts the processor's POST (202 accept-first, 413 over cap rejected BEFORE storing, 503 queue saturated), stores the delivery VERBATIM (raw body bytes base64 -- never re-serialized; headers lowercased + hop-by-hop stripped + dupes ","-joined; the SIGNATURE HEADER TRAVELS untouched), and hands each delivery to the engine poller: GET /deliveries/next claims the oldest claimable (secret-free claim token, per-claim TTL default 120 s -- a dead poller's claim lapses and the delivery reopens, at-least-once transport law), POST /deliveries/<id>/ack records the engine's VERDICT (processed|refused|expired|error, detail <=256 ch; stale/mismatched claim -> 409, delivered-since -> 200 idempotent "already", unknown id -> 404), delivered records STAY as operator audit; GET /deliveries/<id> + /stats are the operator readout; GET /health. THE SECRET LAW: the bridge holds NO HMAC secret and verifies NOTHING -- transport, not armor; PAYMENT_WEBHOOK_SECRET stays in the ENGINE process env and the provider revalidates everything. THE ENGINE POLLER (game/core/economy/webhook_transport.gd, no class_name; booted by NetworkManager._boot_webhook_transport in the prod/server start path ONLY when WEBHOOK_BRIDGE_URL is in the env -- OFF is an honest boot state, clients and dev bots never poll): HTTPRequest legs poll /deliveries/next on a Timer (default 5 s, configure() seam, one request in flight via _busy), feeds the provider board's handle_webhook(RAW bytes, headers) -- the provider resolved through PremiumShopService's registry resolver (injectable resolver for script-mode receipts, runtime load() only, no parse-time preloads) -- and acks the verdict. THE ONCE LAW lives in the engine: a per-process ledger (_handled: delivery_id -> verdict) refuses to re-run the door for a resolved id; a redelivery re-acks the FIRST verdict; NOT call counting on the provider -- the book of record. EVERY deliver_once exit settles BEFORE it answers (ledger writes, then verdict travels: no re-entrant beat can double-run the door). REFUSALS ARE ANSWERS: a refused delivery still acks (a refusal that never acked would replay-wrap forever); verdicts: door complete->processed (order poll leg pays), refused->refused (the armor's own reason rides the record), cancelled->expired, wrong channel->refused unrouted (PAYMENT_WEBHOOK_PROVIDER env is the channel map), provider missing->refused no-provider; transport legs (poll/ack network) retry on the next tick fail-soft. The bridge Python suite (14 tests: FIFO + claim exclusivity, TTL lap-over, ack contract incl. already/unknown, id uniqueness, verbatim bytes round-trip; HTTP: health, the accept->next->ack happy path with signature-header-survives, 413, verdict allowlist, claim-mismatch 409, null views) AND the engine receipt tests/test_webhook_transport.gd (boot law OFF/ON, 5 s default + configure seam, signed-truth delivery routes the door EXACTLY once through the RAW-byte path and pays 100 through the poll leg into a mounted wallet, redelivery re-answers from the ledger, unsigned dies AND settles so a replay cannot wedge, wrong-channel/no-provider refuse by name and settle, a mixed-case signature header survives the normalization and still honors the armor) both exit 0. entrypoint.sh starts the bridge in TEST mode (with its unit suite added to the CI bridge gauntlet) and PROD mode (127.0.0.1:8083, first free port after noaa 8082). DEPLOY.md services table + health list updated. Next due: sigil-sink catalog + OpenHands' staging PRs (gameplay driver A, crafting leg B, perf honesty C).
